## NCT07920094
Comparison of the Safety and Aesthetic Outcomes of a Novel Endoscopic-Assisted Breast-Conserving Surgery (SRRSHzb-EABCS) With Those of Conventional Breast-Conserving Surgery

Intervention: : Endoscopy-assisted breast-conserving surgery (SRRSHzb-EABCS) — A novel gasless endoscopy-assisted breast-conserving surgery using an expansion-traction system (a skin flap dissector and a V-shaped retractor) and a metal wire-based tumor localization system. Through a single 3.0-4.5 cm axillary incision, the retromammary space is created endoscopically. The resection margin is marked 1.5-2.0 cm beyond the tumor edge; U-shaped cutting wires and marking wires are placed percutaneously. The flap is dissected while the V-shaped retractor maintains the working space. Each cutting wire is energized with an electrocautery pencil to cut along the planned margin ("hot-wire" cutting). Margins are assessed by frozen section; positive margins undergo local re-excision, and repeatedly positive margins lead to mastectomy. Axillary management: sentinel lymph node biopsy (SLNB) for clinically node-negative patients, with axillary lymph node dissection (ALND) performed according to biopsy results; clinically node-positive patients undergo ALND directly.
: Conventional open breast-conserving surgery (CBCS) — Conventional open breast-conserving surgery performed according to the Chinese Anti-Cancer Association guidelines for breast-conserving therapy. Generally, separate incisions are made on the breast and in the axilla. The resection includes the tumor together with a margin of surrounding breast tissue. Specimen margins are marked and assessed (inking and/or intraoperative frozen section). Four to six titanium clips are placed in the tumor bed for radiation boost localization. Oncoplastic volume displacement or volume replacement techniques are applied when needed. If a margin is positive on intraoperative or postoperative pathology, local re-excision of the margin is performed, and if margins remain repeatedly positive, mastectomy is performed. Axillary management: sentinel lymph node biopsy (SLNB) for clinically node-negative patients, with axillary lymph node dissection (ALND) performed according to biopsy results; clinically node-positive patients undergo ALND directly.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07920094

SUMMARY:

## NCT07920120
Validation of VR Strategies for Knee Osteoarthritis Rehabilitation

Intervention: : Immersive Virtual Reality Walking Conditions — Participants perform repeated walking trials in four immersive virtual reality (VR) environments presented through a head-mounted display (Vive Focus 3): (1) a neutral environment, (2) a safety-promoting environment on low ground, (3) a fear-inducing environment, and (4) a safety-promoting environment on high ground. Each condition involves walking along a straight 6-meter walkway at a self-selected comfortable speed. At least five valid trials are collected per condition whenever feasible, with a minimum of three valid trials accepted if limited by pain, fatigue, or VR discomfort. The environments are designed to manipulate perceived safety and fear during walking.
: Non-VR Walking — Participants perform overground walking without a virtual reality headset along a straight 6-meter walkway at a self-selected comfortable speed. At least five valid walking trials are collected whenever feasible, with a minimum of three valid trials accepted if limited by pain or fatigue. This condition serves as the control for comparison with the four immersive VR walking conditions.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07920120

SUMMARY:

In situ adjustment and maintenance of the height and angle of the device that allows expansion of the fractured VB in the cranio-caudal direction to realign the vertebral endplates and correct kyphosis. 2. The complex titanium perforated structure with optimized surface roughness that allows possible bone attachment, thereby permitting natural healing of the bone and potentially providing more stability to the device and the treated vertebra over time. 3. ## NCT04941664
Minimum Effective Local Anesthetic Volume of 0.5% Levobupivacaine Required for Ultrasound Guided Superior Trunk Block for Shoulder Surgery

Intervention: : 0.5% levobupivacaine — This study adopts a "Modified Narayana rule (MNR)", a sequential up-and-down pharmacodynamics method (UDM) to estimate the minimum effective local anesthetic volume of 90% (MELVA90) of cases of the aforementioned local anesthetic (LA), in order to produce the surgical anesthesia of the patients scheduled for shoulder surgeries. Based on the principal investigator's clinical experience, the initial volume of 8ml will be used. Depending on the success or failure of the superior trunk block, which is defined as the readiness for surgery at 30 minutes after the block, the volume for the next patient will be adjusted up or down by 2ml. The upper dose limit of the study to minimize the potential for local anesthetic systemic toxicity will be 16ml and the lower dose limit will be 4ml, which will be of no clinical importance.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT04941664

Feasibility and Clinical Utility Study of the Unified Protocol for Group Treatment of Emotional Disorders in Penitentiary Facilities

Intervention: : Unified Protocol — The Unified Protocol (UP) is a transdiagnostic, emotion-focused cognitive-behavioral treatment designed to address a range of emotional disorders, including anxiety, depression, and related conditions. It targets core mechanisms such as emotional avoidance, cognitive rigidity, and maladaptive emotion regulation strategies. The UP consists of structured modules focused on increasing emotional awareness, cognitive flexibility, and exposure to emotionally evocative situations. By addressing common underlying processes across disorders, the UP enhances treatment efficiency and applicability in both individual and group formats. Its flexibility makes it suitable for diverse populations and settings, including community and clinical contexts.

## NCT07393971
Controller Development to Enable Individualized Assistance in Robotic Ankle Exoskeletons

Intervention: : Adaptive Torque Control System for Ankle Exoskeleton — This intervention uses a robotic ankle exoskeleton equipped with a real-time adaptive controller that adjusts plantarflexion torque based on each participant's walking mechanics. Unlike standard exoskeleton controllers that use fixed or pre-programmed assistance levels, this system employs human-in-the-loop optimization to continuously update torque magnitude and timing during treadmill walking. The controller integrates metabolic estimations, kinematic data, and musculoskeletal modeling to identify individualized assistance patterns that reduce walking effort and improve muscle activation efficiency. Participants complete multiple walking trials while the controller automatically modifies assistance to determine the optimal personalized settings.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07393971

## NCT07418034
A Single Arm Phase II Trial in p16-positive Oropharynx Cancer of Selective Dose De-escalation of nodAl VolumEs at Minimal Risk and Primary Site Disease (SAVED)

Intervention: : Dose de-escalation to contralateral neck. — Dose de-escalation of elective treatment volumes including the SAVER-defined volume reduction in the contralateral neck. These patients can be treated in the definitive or adjuvant setting, and have c/pT1-4, N0-1,3 (AJCC 8th ed) disease with a recommendation to treat the contralateral neck. They will undergo treatment with sequential planning of 30Gy to the gross disease and elective neck volumes (using SAVER defined volumes of the contralateral neck) and a subsequent boost to 70Gy for definitive treatment and 50Gy for adjuvant treatment. Chemotherapy will be incorporated per SOC guidelines and based on multi-disciplinary recommendations.
: Dose de-escalation to gross disease to contralateral neck — Dose de-escalation to gross disease for patients treated with definitive chemoRT who meet eligibility for NRG HN 005 (cT1-2N0-2, cT3N0-1 \[AJCC 8th ed\], ≤ 10 Smoking Pack years). Additionally, these patients will require pre-treat positive ctDNA. They will undergo treatment with sequential planning of 30Gy to gross disease and elective neck volumes (using SAVER defined volumes of the contralateral neck). A subsequent boost will be initiated to gross disease. At 4 weeks of treatment, patients will undergo ctDNA analysis. If a patient's ctDNA decreases by ≥95%, final dose will be decreased from SOC dosing for 70Gy to 60Gy (HN-002 dosing). If ctDNA dosing is not resulted by the time patients reach 60Gy, they will continue SOC treatment up to a maximum of 70Gy or until the ctDNA results are available. If there is a ≥95% reduction in ctDNA, treatment will be stopped prior to 70Gy.
: Dose de-escalation to gross disease — Dose de-escalation to the gross disease for patients treated with definitive chemoRT who meet eligibility criteria for NRG HN 005 (cT1-2N0-2, cT3N0-1 \[AJCC 8th ed\], ≤ 10 Smoking Pack years). Additionally, these patients will require pre-treatment positive ctDNA levels. At 4 weeks of treatment (after fraction 19 and before fraction 22), patients will undergo ctDNA analysis. If a patient's ctDNA decreases by ≥95%, final dose will be decreased from SOC dosing for 70Gy to 60Gy (HN-002 dosing). If ctDNA dosing is not resulted by the time patients reach 60Gy, they will continue SOC treatment up to a maximum of 70Gy or until the ctDNA results are available. If there is a ≥95% reduction in ctDNA, treatment will be stopped prior to 70Gy.
: Circulating Tumor DNA test (ctDNA test) — Blood test for diagnostic and surveillance purposes measuring expression of Cell free HPV tumor DNA (ctDNA) in the blood. Patients who are eligible for dose de-escalation to gross disease, whether they are also eligible for dose de-escalation of contralateral neck or not, will undergo ctDNA evaluation pretreatment, at 4 weeks of treatment, and then at 3, 6, and 12 months post treatment.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07418034

SUMMARY:

## NCT07458048
Comparıson of the Effıcacy of Conventıonal Exercıse and Pılates ın Pre-fraıl and Fraıl Older Adults

Intervention: : PILATES EXERCISES GROUP — The Pilates-based exercise program is structured and supervised by a physiotherapist. The program includes controlled movements, postural alignment, core stabilization, breathing control, and progressive overload principles. Exercise intensity is individualized based on perceived exertion, targeting moderate intensity (Borg CR10 scale: 4-6). The program will be structured as 20 minutes of rest and 40 minutes of exercise applications to maintain participant tolerance.

Pilates is a safe and adaptable therapeutic modality for the elderly. Adjustable components like the foot bar and headrest allow for ergonomic modifications tailored to physical capacity and joint morphology. This customization facilitates controlled execution for individuals with mobility impairments. Diverse movement variations and graded progression enable program diversification. Furthermore, controlled breathing enhances trunk stability and rhythmic execution.
: CONVENTIONAL EXERCISES GROUP — The conventional exercise program is supervised by a physiotherapist and consists of range of motion exercises, stretching, strengthening exercises targeting major muscle groups, and balance training. Exercise intensity is individualized and maintained at moderate intensity. Sessions are conducted twice weekly for six weeks, approximately 60 minutes per session.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07458048

## NCT07617584
Near-Infrared Autofluorescence-Guided Parathyroid Identification During Thyroidectomy

Intervention: : Wearable Near-Infrared Autofluorescence Imaging System — A modified Meta Quest head-mounted stereoscopic goggle system configured for label-free near-infrared autofluorescence imaging. The system delivers near-infrared excitation at approximately 780 nm and detects parathyroid autofluorescence centered near 800 nm. No injected dye or external contrast agent is administered. The device is used briefly after thyroid exposure for in vivo mapping and after thyroid resection for ex vivo specimen interrogation.

## NCT07650448
WATER IV Prostate Cancer: Aquablation Versus Active Surveillance for the Treatment of Localized Prostate Cancer

Intervention: : Aquablation Therapy — The AquaBeam Robotic System and the HYDROS Robotic System utilize high-velocity sterile saline waterjet to resect prostate tissue, guided by real-time visualization through cystoscopy and transrectal ultrasound imaging. This minimally invasive surgical procedure is called the Aquablation therapy.
: Active Surveillance — Active surveillance is a management strategy for men with low- and favorable intermediate-risk localized prostate cancer that involves close monitoring with curative intent, reserving treatment for evidence of disease progression. Monitoring typically includes serial PSA testing, periodic prostate MRI, and repeat biopsies at scheduled intervals, with intervention triggered by evidence of disease progression.

## NCT07680712
Comparative Effects of Yoga Versus Stretching-Strengthening Exercises on Physical Fitness in Sedentary Young Adults

Intervention: : yoga group — The yoga group will participate in a structured beginner-level yoga program consisting of two 50-minute sessions per week for six weeks. All sessions will be conducted under the supervision of the researchers. Each session will comprise three primary phases:

Preparation and Breath Awareness: 5 minutes Asana Flow: 40 minutes Deep Relaxation and Mindfulness: 5 minutes Throughout the intervention, the exercise intensity and difficulty level will be increased progressively.
: stretching-strengthening group — The stretching-strengthening group will participate in a structured exercise program consisting of two 50-minute sessions per week for six weeks. The exercises will be performed under the supervision of the researchers. The program will comprise core stabilization, upper and lower extremity strengthening, and stretching phases.

Throughout the intervention, the exercise intensity and difficulty level will be increased progressively; progression to the next level will occur when a participant can exceed the target number of repetitions without compromising proper form.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07680712

## NCT07706543
Evaluation of a Therapeutic Patient Education Program for Discharge (ETPsh) by Nurses in a French Hospital Setting Among Patients With Heart Failure

Intervention: : Nurse-led discharge education — The intervention includes :

1. An initial educational assessment (≈1 h) on the day after enrollment to identify individualized learning needs for discharge and assess key self-management and safety competencies ;
2. An interactive discharge education session (≈1 h) within 24 hours before discharge, using the teach-back method to reinforce warning signs, medication, self-care, follow-up, and healthcare contacts ;
3. A nurse-led follow-up telephone call 7 (±3) days after discharge (15-30 min) to assess competencies and reinforce learning if needed ;
4. A final educational assessment (≈1 h) during the routine 30-day (±7 days) follow-up consultation. Informal caregivers will be invited to participate whenever possible.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07706543

## NCT07760779
Addressing Nocturia in Older Women Utilizing Behavioral Sleep Intervention and Brief Mindfulness

Intervention: : Brief behavioral treatment of insomnia — BBTI: will entail a 45- to 60-minute individual intervention session with instructions regarding sleep education and discussion of homeostatic and circadian mechanisms of human sleep regulation. This education provides the rationale for the 4 main interventions of BBTI:

1. reduce time in bed;
2. get up at the same time every day, regardless of sleep duration;
3. do not go to bed unless sleepy; and
4. do not stay in bed unless asleep
: MI — Participants in the MI group will be trained to engage in a brief mindfulness method involving body scan with acceptance language training.
: Control — The control condition is intended to emulate the behavioral treatment information generally available to patients.
: BBTI+MI — Participants in this arm will receive BBTI as well as MI training.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07760779

## NCT07766213
Pain Phenotyping in Multiple Sclerosis Based on IASP Criteria

Intervention: : Pain classification method — A current clinical algorithm will be used to determine the predominant type of pain in individuals with MS. The classification to diagnose nociceptive, neuropathic and nociplastic pain will based on a recent method developed by Nijs et al. This method consists of seven steps in total. These steps question the following, respectively: duration of pain, pain distribution, presence of nociceptive pain, presence of neuropathic pain, phenomenon of hypersensitivity, presence of hypersensitization and presence of specific comorbidity. Besides, inter-rater and intra-rater reliability of pain classification algorithm will be determined by two independent researchers.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07766213

## NCT07790835
Cultural Adaptation and Randomized Controlled Trial Evaluation of an Online Parenting Intervention to Reduce the Risk of Depression and Anxiety in Adolescents

Intervention: : Partners in Parenting — The Partners in Parenting (PiP) intervention is a fully automated, web-based parenting program comprising three components. First, parents complete a self-assessment scale, the Parenting to Reduce Adolescent Depression and Anxiety Scale (PRADAS), which evaluates their current parenting practices against established Guidelines. Second, based on PRADAS responses, parents receive an individually tailored feedback report highlighting their parenting strengths and areas for improvement. The feedback is designed to be concise, encourage behavior change, provide practical parenting strategies, and include links to additional resources if desired. Third, parents are recommended a series of interactive online modules, personalized according to their PRADAS responses. Nine modules are available in total, and parents can further tailor their program by selecting additional modules not initially recommended or opting out of recommended ones.
: Shamiri — group-based mental health program designed for adolescents to reduce depression and anxiety. This intervention is derived from the Growth Mindset Single Session Intervention (GM-SSI) which has demonstrated effectiveness for a range of psychological problems of adolescents, including depression and anxiety, as well as other distress-related difficulties. Evidence indicates that GM-SSI can serve as a low-cost approach to enhance youth resilience and mental health. The shamiri is mindset program comprising: a growth mindset module (2 sessions), a gratitude module (1 session), and a values affirmation module (1 session). Sessions are delivered weekly, each lasting approximately one hour.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07790835

SUMMARY:

: Pembrolizumab and Berahyaluronidase alfa — Pembrolizumab coformulated with berahyaluronidase alfa administered as a SC injection

Rescue medications include pegfilgrastim (or equivalent), H1 receptor antagonist, H2 receptor antagonist, acetaminophen (or equivalent), dexamethasone (or equivalent), steroid mouthwash (dexamethasone or equivalent).

## NCT07843602
The Effect of Cyclic Sighing Breathing Exercise on Catheter-Related Bladder Discomfort, Pain, and Sleep Quality in Patients Undergoing Transurethral Resection: A Randomized Controlled Trial

Intervention: : Cyclic Sighing Breathing Exercise — The cyclic sighing intervention consists of structured breathing sessions taught preoperatively and supervised postoperatively. The patient assumes a comfortable supine or semi-Fowler position in bed. The exercise involves taking a slow, deep inhalation through the nose, immediately followed by a second short nasal inhalation to achieve maximum lung expansion, and then slowly and fully exhaling through the mouth, ensuring that exhalation lasts longer than inhalation. Patients perform this cycle continuously for 5 minutes at the 1st, 2nd, 6th, 12th, and 24th postoperative hours under researcher supervision. To protect nocturnal sleep patterns, sessions scheduled after 22:00 will be completed by 22:00.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07843602

## NCT07860723
REACH Study: Reproductive Empowerment for Adolescents Through CAHLS and Health Services (FPIL Sierra Leone Component)

Intervention: : Comprehensive Sexuality Education — For junior secondary schools in the "CSE only" and "CSE+mobile SRH services" intervention arms, at baseline at the start of the school year, MSSL will train teachers who teach subjects in which CSE topics are included, using a standard teaching training curriculum to help mitigate the risk of variability in the quality of CSE implementation across schools. Teachers will then implement the CSE curriculum in their classes throughout the duration of the study. MSSL will provide yearly refresher teacher training throughout the study. In each school, guidance counselors will be tasked with monitoring implementation of the intervention. MSSL will provide all materials and equipment needed to teach CSE. Each school will create a "safe space" where students can go for menstrual hygiene and care.
: Mobile SRH services — For junior secondary schools in the "Mobile SRH services only" and "CSE+mobile SRH services" intervention arms, MSSL will utilize three mobile clinical teams to provide direct, comprehensive SRHR services on-site at schools. Each team will be composed of two service providers trained to provide youth-friendly services, one data imputer, and a transport assistant. The team will visit each school once per quarter (3 times during the school year). A comprehensive range of youth-friendly SRH services, including contraceptive counseling, provision of LARC (implants and IUDs), short-term methods (pills, injectables and emergency contraception), condom promotion, STI services, and referrals to health facilities if needed, will be available to all students through the mobile visits.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07860723

Evaluation of the Relationship Between Preoperative Gastric Ultrasonography Findings and Left Ventricular Systolic Function in Patients Undergoing Elective Coronary Artery Bypass Graft Surgery

## NCT07910773
Guiding Optimal Blood Pressure Management in Peritoneal Dialysis Pilot Trial

Intervention: : Target systolic blood pressure <120 mmHg. — The target SBP \<120 mmHg will be operationalized as SBP 100-119 mmHg in the intervention arm.

For volume control, the patient's dry weight target will be adjusted downward and will be attempted to be achieved through greater PD ultrafiltration, dietary fluid/salt restriction, or diuretics.

Once patients are deemed euvolemic based on the assessment of the treating nephrologist, antihypertensive medications will be added or adjusted in a standardized sequence: priority is given to angiotensin-converting enzyme inhibitors (ACE inhibitors) or angiotensin receptor blockers (ARBs) due to data about this class of agents being shown to better preserve residual kidney function. Other medications are recommended to be added as indicated to maintain SBP \<120 mmHg in the interventional arm.

If SBP were \<100 mmHg at one visit or \<105 mmHg at two consecutive visits, BP medications will be reduced to keep SBP in the range of 100 to 119 mmHg, to avoid hypotensive episodes.
: Target systolic blood pressure <140 mmHg — The target SBP \<140 mmHg will be operationalized as SBP 130-139 mmHg. For volume control, the patient's dry weight target will be adjusted downward and will be attempted to be achieved through greater PD ultrafiltration, dietary fluid/salt restriction, or diuretics. Once patients are deemed euvolemic based on the assessment of the treating nephrologist, antihypertensive medications will be added or adjusted in a standardized sequence: priority is given to angiotensin-converting enzyme inhibitors (ACE inhibitors) or angiotensin receptor blockers (ARBs) due to data about this class of agents being shown to better preserve residual kidney function. Other medications are recommended to be added as indicated to maintain \<140 mmHg in the control arm. If SBP were \<130 mmHg at one visit or \<135 mmHg at two consecutive visits, BP medications will be tapered/discontinued to keep SBP in the range of 130 to 139 mmHg.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07910773

SUMMARY:

## NCT07914790
Effect of Epidural Hydroxyethyl Starch Administration for Prevention of Post-Dural Puncture Headache in Parturients: A Multi-center Prospective Cohort Study

Intervention: : Epidural hydroxyethyl starch — For parturients with accidental dural puncture during labor epidural analgesia, after successful reinsertion of the epidural catheter at a different interspace, 15 mL of hydroxyethyl starch (HES) will be administered into the epidural space at two time points: the first at 30 minutes after the initial epidural dose administered through the reinserted catheter, and the second before catheter removal 24 hours after delivery.

For parturients with accidental dural puncture during cesarean delivery, 15 mL of HES will be administered into the epidural space at two time points: after delivery of the fetus and before catheter removal at the end of the 48-hour analgesia period.
: No epidural hydroxyethyl starch — For parturients with accidental dural puncture during labor epidural analgesia, after successful reinsertion of the epidural catheter at a different interspace, no HES will be administered into the epidural space in the control group. The corresponding time points in the intervention group are 30 minutes after the initial epidural dose administered through the reinserted catheter and before catheter removal 24 hours after delivery.

For parturients with accidental dural puncture during cesarean delivery, no HES will be administered into the epidural space in the control group. The corresponding time points in the intervention group are after delivery of the fetus and before catheter removal at the end of the 48-hour analgesia period.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07914790

SUMMARY:

## NCT07921004
Effects of Alexander Technique Versus Feldenkrais Somatic Training on Pain, Cranio-Vertebral Angle and Disability in Patients With Text Neck Syndrome

Intervention: : Alexander Technique — Each patient will receive a total number of eight treatment sessions over a four-week period and each session is about 45 min. There will be 3 sessions in per week. Constructive rest-5 min, Gentle cervical rotation in supine , 4-5 each side, Head and neck release awareness in sitting, Semi supine arm awareness, Sitting Posture Re-education: Ask the patient to hold the phone at eye level, Sitting to standing by Alexander Method, Gentle cervical rotation sitting, Functional Reaching with AT Cues, Stimulated phone use training, Sustained posture awareness drill
: Feldenkrais Somatic training — Each patient will receive a total number of eight treatment sessions over a four-week period and each session is about 45 min. There will be 3 sessions in per week. Body scan in supine, small circles in supine , Chin nodding in supine lying, Jaw-neck awareness (ask the patient to open \& close mouth gently), Head turning with shoulder awareness (patient is in supine position, ask the patient to turn head right while noticing left shoulder and move to the alternate sides.), Eye-head coordination (ask the patient to move eyes right first, then head follows then reverse direction.), Side-lying head lift (1 cm), Scapula-neck relationship (patient in standing, ask the patient to gently slide shoulder up/down while head rests and therapist observe neck response.), Thoracic rolling in supine, Pelvic rocking with in supine lying, Standing weight shift + head response, Walking with free neck, Text neck re education
: Base line Treatment (Standard PT protocol) — TENS (10 min), Hot pack (10 min), Soft tissue mobilization, Grade I, II matland mobilization of cervical spine, AROM (10 reps- 2 sets), Upper trapezius stretch (5 sets, hold for 15-30 sec), Levator Scapulae stretch (5 sets, hold for 15-30 sec), SCM stretch (5 sets, hold for 15-30 sec), Pectoralis major stretch (5 sets, hold for 15-30 sec), Cervical isometrics in flexion, extension and side bending (5-10 sets, hold for 10 sec).

Effects of Kendall Versus McKenzie Exercises on Pain, Functional Disability, and Craniovertebral Angle in Patients With Upper Crossed Syndrome

Static stretching of levator scapulae 5 repetitions per side; hold 30 seconds; rest 30 seconds between repetitions.

PA maitland glide cervical and thoracic t1-t3

## NCT07921108
Effects of Mobile Device Delivered Action Observation Therapy and Mirror-based Action Observation Therapy for Post- Stroke Telerehabilitation: A Randomized Control Trial

Intervention: : App-based combined action observation and mirror therapy — Participants receive a 4-week telerehabilitation regimen delivered via the "Neuro-Mirror Pro" mobile web application, consisting of 36 minutes per daily session, 5 days per week, in addition to regular conventional occupational therapy (1 hour daily on weekdays). The intervention integrates action observation therapy with client-side mirrored visual feedback. Participants observe standardized video demonstrations of 12 upper extremity functional movements while actively performing the movements. Real-time visual feedback is rendered locally using client-side WebRTC and CSS horizontal inversion, creating the visual illusion that the paretic arm is moving smoothly, thereby stimulating motor cortex reorganization without transmitting raw video data to external servers.
: App-based action observation therapy — Participants receive a 4-week telerehabilitation regimen delivered via the "Neuro-Mirror Pro" mobile web application, consisting of 36 minutes per daily session, 5 days per week, in addition to regular conventional occupational therapy (1 hour daily on weekdays). The intervention consists of action observation therapy alone. Participants observe standardized video demonstrations of 12 upper extremity functional movements and actively perform the observed actions without mirrored visual feedback. Training adherence and completion are recorded client-side to ensure compliance and privacy without cloud video storage.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07921108

SUMMARY:

## NCT07921212
Effects of Telerehabilitation Versus Supervised Rehabilitation Protocol on Pain, Range of Motion and Functional Recovery in Patients With Patellofemoral Pain Syndrome.

Intervention: : Telerehabilitation — The telerehabilitation intervention consists of a structured rehabilitation program delivered remotely through virtual communication platforms. The program includes therapeutic exercises, stretching, strengthening, and functional training designed to reduce pain, improve knee range of motion, and enhance functional recovery in patients with Patellofemoral Pain Syndrome. Participants will receive regular guidance, monitoring, and progression of exercises through remote sessions. Participants receive a structured 6-week telerehabilitation exercise program delivered remotely under live, real-time video call supervision by a licensed physical therapist (3 sessions per week, total 18 sessions, for 40 mins.

Baseline Protocol 5-10 minutes warm-up walk.10-15 minutes self-applied moist hot pack over anterior knee. Self-stretching exercises for Hamstrings, Quadriceps, Gastrocnemius-Soleus, and Iliotibial band (3 repetitions of 30-second holds each).Pain-free active knee range of motion exercises
: Supervised Rehabilitation — Participants receive face-to-face, in-clinic supervised physical therapy delivered directly by a licensed physical therapist (3 sessions per week ,40 min,total 18 sessions).

Baseline Protocol (Pre-Exercise):

5-10 minutes warm-up walk. 10-15 minutes hot pack surrounding patella). Therapist-assisted passive/active-assisted stretching for Hamstrings, Quadriceps, Gastrocnemius-Soleus, and Iliotibial band (3 repetitions of 20-second holds each).

Pain-free active and active-assisted knee range of motion (flexion and extension) exercises.

Exercise Progression Protocol:

Identical exercise progression framework as the Telerehabilitation group (Weeks 1 to 6 covering SLR, hip abduction, clamshells, squats, lunges, step-ups, step-downs, and single-leg balance), performed in-clinic under direct hands-on physical supervision and guidance.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07921212

SUMMARY:

Intervention: : Control - aPDT Without Additional Activation — All participants will receive antimicrobial photodynamic therapy (aPDT) using methylene blue as the photosensitizer, followed by photodynamic light irradiation with the FotoSan device. The groups will differ according to the intracanal activation technique applied to methylene blue.
: Passive Ultrasonic Irrigation (PUI) — Methylene blue will be activated within the root canal using passive ultrasonic irrigation before photodynamic light irradiation with the FotoSan device.
: Standard Needle Irrigation (SNI) — Methylene blue will be agitated within the root canal using a standard irrigation needle with back-and-forth movements before photodynamic light irradiation with the FotoSan device.
: Laser-Activated Irrigation (LAI) — ethylene blue will be activated within the root canal using an Er,Cr:YSGG laser-assisted irrigation technique before photodynamic light irradiation with the FotoSan device

Generative Artificial Intelligence Assisted Home-based Intervention for Attention Deficient Hyperactivity Disorder (ADHD) Children

Intervention: : Generative artificial intelligence assisted diary platform home-based intervention — Participants will use the MangaChat diary platform to log their daily moods and events, which the platform transforms into comic strips to offer an engaging visual story of their emotional experiences. Participants are requested to use the platform daily (or at least 3 times per week) for approximately 10 to 15 minutes per session over a 12-week period. Prior to using the platform, a 1-hour online orientation session will be provided to guide parents on how to support their child's emotional management at home using the digital tool.

These sessions will focus on discussing real-life situations and establishing coping strategies. (If parents express interest in using the online platform, free access equivalent to the 12-week intervention given to the platform group will be provided after study completion, ensuring their rights and benefits are fully preserved regardless of group assignment.)

Intervention: : Transcutaneous spinal cord stimulation — The intervention consists of noninvasive transcutaneous spinal cord stimulation (TSS) delivered using the ANEUVO ExaStim Stimulation System in combination with intensive, task-specific physical and occupational therapy in adults with chronic spinal cord injury who are receiving physician-prescribed dalfampridine (4-aminopyridine).

Stimulation will be delivered through surface electrodes positioned over the cervical (C5-C7) and thoracolumbar (T11-L1) spinal regions, with abdominal reference electrodes. The stimulation waveform will consist of biphasic rectangular pulses, with pulse widths ranging from 500 to 2000 microseconds and frequencies of 0-100 Hz. Stimulation intensity will be individualized to produce visible motor activation without discomfort.

Participants will be randomized to receive one of two doses:

Higher-dose group: 30 mL of 1% lidocaine (300 mg), combined with 0.5 mg of adrenaline and 20 mL of 0.9% sodium chloride solution.

## NCT07923279
Effects of a Prebiotic-Probiotic Combination on Gastrointestinal Symptoms and Quality of Life in Individuals With Constipation: A Randomized, Double-Blind, Controlled Trial

Intervention: : Lacticaseibacillus rhamnosus BBH112 — Lacticaseibacillus rhamnosus BBH112 is a probiotic strain isolated from a healthy human donor. The probiotic is administered orally as two capsules once daily for 8 weeks. Each capsule contains 1 × 10\^10 colony-forming units (CFU) of L. rhamnosus BBH112, providing a total daily dose of 2 × 10\^10 CFU. Participants in both the experimental and active comparator groups will receive this probiotic supplementation.
: 1-Kestose (GF2) — 1-Kestose (GF2) is a prebiotic fructooligosaccharide with a purity of at least 98%. Participants assigned to the experimental group will receive 1-kestose powder orally at a dose of 5 g twice daily (total daily dose of 10 g) for 8 weeks, in combination with Lacticaseibacillus rhamnosus BBH112. The intervention aims to evaluate the additional effects of prebiotic supplementation on constipation-related gastrointestinal symptoms, bowel habits, quality of life, and gut microbiome composition.
: Dextrin Placebo Powder — Participants assigned to the active comparator group will receive placebo powder orally at a dose of 5 g twice daily (total daily dose of 10 g) for 8 weeks, in combination with Lacticaseibacillus rhamnosus BBH112. The placebo will be administered under blinded conditions to allow comparison with the prebiotic intervention.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07923279

## NCT07923318
Randomized Controlled Study of Optimal Treatment Strategy for Spontaneous Bacterial Peritonitis in Liver Cirrhosis: A Pilot Study

Intervention: : Routine Follow-up Paracentesis at 48 Hours — Participants assigned to the routine follow-up paracentesis arm will undergo repeat paracentesis approximately 48 hours after initiation of antibiotic therapy for spontaneous bacterial peritonitis. Ascitic fluid will be analyzed for polymorphonuclear leukocyte (PMN) count, bacterial culture and antimicrobial susceptibility, and biochemical parameters. If the ascitic fluid PMN count has not decreased by at least 25% from baseline, antibiotic therapy may be escalated according to the study protocol. Standard antibiotic therapy and intravenous albumin will be administered according to the study protocol.
: Other — Participants will not undergo scheduled follow-up paracentesis at approximately 48 hours. Treatment response will be assessed based on clinical symptoms, vital signs, and blood inflammatory markers. Paracentesis may be performed if clinically indicated, such as for worsening abdominal symptoms, persistent fever, or hemodynamic instability. Standard antibiotic therapy and intravenous albumin will be administered according to the study protocol.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07923318

## NCT07923370
The FEATURE Study: Facial Expression Awareness Training to Understand and Recognize Emotions

Intervention: : FaceSay — FaceSay (http://www.facesay.com/) is a computer-delivered, software program to enhance face processing in youth, a core component of social information processing (SIP). The training occurs in a game-like environment that repeatedly challenges different aspects of face processing. FaceSay contains three different games that target various skills. "Amazing Gazing" targets users' eye gaze and joint attention and requires users to respond based on the game character's eye gaze. In the game "Band Aid Clinic," users reconstruct a game character's face by applying the correct band aid that includes pictures of facial features. This game promotes processing of facial expressions through different facial features and configurations. The final game, "Follow the Leader," requires users to match facial expressions from one game character to another. As the user advances, the facial expressions become increasingly subtle and more challenging.
: SuccessMaker Reading — SuccessMaker: SuccessMaker is an online, academic skills building program. Using adaptive lessons tailored to the participant's reading level, SuccessMaker aims to enhance understanding in phonological awareness, phonics, fluency, vocabulary, comprehension, and concepts of print. The program adapts to the performance of the user across areas to introduce new skills when appropriate to the user. SuccessMaker targets different domains than by FaceSay and those not associated with social difficulties. SuccessMaker uses adaptive lessons tailored to the participant's reading level to enhance understanding in phonological awareness, phonics, fluency, vocabulary, comprehension, and concepts of print. The program adapts to the performance of the user across areas to introduce new skills when appropriate to the user.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07923370

SUMMARY:

## NCT06425510
Tech Enabled Functional Health: Bridging Primary Care Gaps for Older Latinos With Functional Disabilities in Underserved Communities

Intervention: : Translation: Living the Advantages of Technological Assistance (VIVE-AT) — It comprises 2-hour small group sessions of 8-10 participants, once a week, for six weeks of participatory discussions, experiential learning, multimodal instructions, and demonstration and practice with selected assistive technology devices (ATDs). The content of the VIVE-AT weekly sessions is as follows: Week 1 - Introduction to ATDs, funding, and resources; Week 2 - ATDs for self-care and toilet use; Week 3 - ATDs for mobility; Week 4 - ATDs for dressing; Week 6 - ATDs for cooking and home tasks. Each session is designed with the following components: monitoring of participants\&#39; weekly goals; providing information on ATDs, resources, and services; reflection on the advantages and disadvantages of using these ATDs; hands-on practice with selected ATDs; goal setting and addressing barriers to using ATDs. Group sessions will incorporate visual aids, including modeling and videos of older individuals using ATDs accessed through an AT web app in a tablet provided by this project.
: Attention calls — Ten minutes of attention calls, once a week for six weeks, to the waitlist control participants will offer general health advice on topics like nutrition, exercise, sleep, stress, and social connections. These calls emphasize participant well-being without touching upon the specific VIVE-AT content, maintaining a clear distinction from the intervention group.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06425510

## NCT07376395
The Effect of Jin Shin Jyutsu-Based Hand Application Guided by the Transactional Model of Stress and Coping on Quality of Life and Psychosocial Status in Perimenopausal Women

Intervention: : General Health Education — Participants in both the experimental and control groups will receive standardized verbal health education related to perimenopause and menopause. The content includes information on common perimenopausal and menopausal changes and general lifestyle recommendations. This education is provided in the same standardized format to both groups. No Jin Shin Jyutsu® techniques, relaxation exercises, or structured coping interventions are included as part of the health education itself.
: Jin Shin Jyutsu®-Based Hand Application — Participants assigned to the experimental arm will receive a Jin Shin Jyutsu®-based hand application program guided by the Transactional Model of Stress and Coping. During the initial face-to-face session, the researcher will provide verbal and visual instruction and demonstrate the hand application technique. Participants will then perform the technique under researcher observation as a return-demonstration, and correct performance will be confirmed before independent practice. Participants will be instructed to perform the standardized hand application for 5 minutes daily over a 14-day period. The application will be performed according to the standardized protocol using two natural breathing cycles. Written instructions, a daily self-monitoring log, and brief reminder messages via WhatsApp® will be used to support adherence. The intervention is a self-care-based supportive approach and does not involve medical treatment, physical manipulation, or pharmacological therapy.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07376395

SUMMARY:

## NCT07391267
Pathogenesis of Chronic Kidney Disease Associated With Metabolic Dysfunction- Associated Fatty Liver Disease (MAFLD) and Treatment Response of Oral Semaglutide - a Randomized Controlled Trial.

Intervention: : Semaglutide Oral Tablet — GLP1-R agonists GLP1-R agonists (GLP1-RAs) are novel potent antidiabetic agents with proven efficacy in reducing major adverse cardiovascular events. Besides their glucose-lowering action, their beneficial hepatic effects may be related to the influence on the AMPK/mTOR pathway. Semaglutide was associated with significant decreases in body weight, alanine aminotransferase, liver steatosis, and stiffness.GLP1-RAs may also improve histologic features on NAFLD, such as liver fat deposition, steatohepatitis, and fibrosis. GLP1-RAs have shown benefits in preventing the development or halting the progression of CKD. It also promotes antioxidative and anti-inflammatory actions may be among the determining factors in this renoprotective effect, together with weight loss, blood pressure, and glucose-lowering.
: Placebo — Placebo will be given in the same manner.
: Standard medical treatment — Standard medical treatment-

1. Lifestyle first - weight loss , caloric restriction, increased aerobic + resistance exercise, treat obesity and metabolic syndrome.
2. Optimize blood-pressure control and use RAAS blockade when indicated (ACE inhibitor or ARB) to reduce albuminuria and slow CKD progression .
3. Treat dysglycaemia and favour drug classes with kidney + liver benefit when appropriate

   * SGLT2 inhibitors (dapagliflozin, empagliflozin, canagliflozin) - recommended for people with T2D + CKD
   * GLP-1 receptor agonists (e.g., semaglutide)
4. Lipid management / statins - treat according to CV risk
5. Consider established liver-directed agents when appropriate
6. Pioglitazone (thiazolidinedione) and vitamin-E
7. Standard CKD supportive care - salt and fluid management, correction of metabolic acidosis, anaemia and mineral bone abnormalities as per CKD stage; immunize and address CV risk factors aggressively
8. Avoid hepatotoxic drugs / review medications
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07391267

SUMMARY:

## NCT07544355
A Mindfulness-Based Stress Reduction Training Program Model for Empowering Nursing Students

Intervention: : The mindfulness group-"Mindfulness-Based Stress Reduction Program" — Week 1: Introduction, defining group dynamics, establishing group rules. Week 2: What is mindfulness? The breathing exercise, autopilot, raisin eating exercise.

Homework: Creating mindfulness routines (brushing teeth, walking, showering, cooking or eating).

Week 3: Stress, the physiology of stress, and the relationship between mindfulness and stress. Sitting meditation.

Homework: Doing sitting meditation twice a week. Week 4: The relationship between thought, emotion, and behavior, Body scan meditation. Homework: Doing body scan meditation one day, sitting meditation the next. Doing 10-finger exercises.

Week 5: Coping with challenging emotions and self-compassion. Self-compassion meditation.

Homework: Caring for a plant or planting a flower. One day body scan, one day self-compassion meditation.

Week 6: Program evaluation and closing.
: The life skills workshop group-"Lifestyle Management and Well-being Workshop" — Week 1: Introduction, defining group dynamics. Week 2: Physiological Relaxation (Progressive Relaxation Exercises) Week 3: Sleep Hygiene: Circadian Rhythm and Biological Clock, Melatonin and Light Relationship, Sleep Stages and Restorative Effect Week 4: Nutrition and Stress: The effect of nutrition on stress and anxiety will be discussed: Blood Sugar Balance and "False Anxiety" (Glycemic Index), Gut-Brain Axis and Serotonin, Caffeine and Cortisol Interaction.

Week 5: Social Support and Problem Solving: Time Management and Academic Stress (Problem-Oriented Coping), Social Support and Oxytocin Effect, and Recreation and "Active Rest".

Week 6: Program evaluation and closing.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07544355

Intervention: : Full MISSION-CJ — MISSION-CJ is a time-limited, cross disciplinary, team-based wraparound approach that provides 6 months of psychosocial treatment combined with assertive outreach, empowering clients to access and engage in care and community services to promote recovery and address criminogenic risk. The MISSION-CJ treatment curriculum integrates 3 evidence-based practices: 1) Critical Time Intervention (CTI), a time-limited form of assertive community treatment; 2) Dual Recovery Therapy (DRT), which is integrated mental health and substance use group therapy; and 3) Peer Support (PS), offering support for people in recovery by people in recovery.

## NCT07577687
The Effect of Emg-Biofeedback Based Relaxation Training Added to a Multimodal Exercise Program on Pain and Function in Patients With Temporomandibular Joint Dysfunction

Intervention: : Multimodal physiotherapy program — A comprehensive physiotherapy program consisting of manual therapy and home exercises will be implemented for 5 weeks (multimodal exercises will be performed by a physiotherapist in the clinic one day a week, and home exercises will be performed by the patient at home on the other two days of the week).
: EMG-Biofeedback — The EMG-Biofeedback program will include relaxation-based video games to reduce muscle activation. In addition to the multimodal physiotherapy program (manual therapy + home exercises), 20 minutes of relaxation training with EMG-Biofeedback will be provided. EMG-Biofeedback will be applied bilaterally to the masseter, temporalis, sternoclavicular carotid artery (SCM), and upper trapezius muscles. For each muscle, superficial electrode placement will be done on the muscle body along the direction of the muscle fibers. Electrode placement will be performed bilaterally with participants seated in a back-supported chair, feet on the floor, head and cervical region in neutral alignment, and mandible in a resting position (without teeth touching). Each muscle will receive 5 minutes of application.

## NCT07607691
Evaluation of the Analgesic Efficacy and Functional Outcomes of Ultrasound-Guided PENG Block in Chronic Shoulder Pain

Intervention: : Lidocaine and Dexamethasone for PENG Block — An ultrasound-guided Pericapsular Nerve Group (PENG) block is administered to target the articular (sensory) branches of the nerves innervating the shoulder joint capsule (suprascapular, axillary, and lateral pectoral nerves) without affecting the motor branches.

The intervention is performed under strict sterile conditions with the patient in the supine position. The affected shoulder is placed in abduction and external rotation. A high-frequency ultrasound transducer is positioned parallel to the clavicle to visualize the anatomical structures. Under direct ultrasound guidance, a block needle is advanced beneath the subscapularis muscle. After confirming the correct needle positioning and ensuring no intravascular placement, a therapeutic mixture of 5 mL of 2% lidocaine and 8 mg of dexamethasone is injected into the periarticular nerve endpoints.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07607691

SUMMARY:

Routine Microaxial Flow Pump Versus Radial Access Revascularization Without Routine Microaxial Flow Pump in Infarct-Related Cardiogenic Shock & Routine Pulmonary Artery Catheterization-based Monitoring With Protocolized Hemodynamic Optimization Versus Simplified Monitoring Without Protocolized Hemodynamic Optimization in Infarct-Related Cardiogenic Shock

Intervention: : Microaxial flow pump (left ventricular assist device) — Percutaneous implantation of a left-sided microaxial flow pump via the femoral artery following PCI. The device actively unloads the left ventricle by aspirating blood from the left ventricle and ejecting it into the ascending aorta, thereby augmenting cardiac output. Implantation occurs immediately after PCI.
: Pulmonary artery catheter — Insertion of a pulmonary artery catheter via central venous access (jugular, subclavian, or femoral vein) for continuous hemodynamic monitoring and protocol-based optimization of cardiovascular function. Measured parameters include cardiac output, pulmonary capillary wedge pressure (PCWP), and systemic and pulmonary vascular resistance.
: Conventional hemodynamic monitoring — Standard intensive care hemodynamic monitoring without pulmonary artery catheter, including invasive arterial blood pressure measurement, central venous pressure monitoring, echocardiography, and serial laboratory parameters (e.g., lactate, creatinine, liver enzymes, blood count).
: Hemodynamic stabilization — Hemodynamic stabilization without hemodynamic protocol by pulmonary artery catheter. Hemodynamic stabilization through intravenous vasoactive agents, including vasopressors (e.g., norepinephrine) and/or inotropes (e.g., dobutamine), administered according to current clinical guidelines. Dosage and duration are determined by the treating physician based on hemodynamic response. In case of refractory cardiogenic shock unresponsive to medical therapy, escalation to mechanical circulatory support is permitted at the discretion of the treating physician.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07762560

SUMMARY:

## NCT07801703
Optimizing the Synergy Between Behavioural Activation Therapy and Intravenous Ketamine for Treatment-Resistant Depression

Intervention: : Ketamine (0.5 mg/kg) — IV ketamine will be administered under medical supervision at a fixed dose of 0.5 mg/kg infused over 40 minutes. Treatments will be administered twice weekly for three weeks during the Induction Phase (6 treatments). Participants' response to ketamine will be assessed after the Induction Phase. Those who do not meet response criteria (\<50% reduction in MADRS score) will be deemed Nonresponders and will conclude receiving ketamine at that time. Those who meet the response criteria (≥50% reduction in MADRS score) will be deemed Responders and will proceed to the Maintenance Phase. During the Maintenance Phase, ketamine treatments will be administered once weekly for 8 weeks (8 treatments), followed by once biweekly for four weeks during the Discharge Preparation Phase (2 treatments). Responders will receive a total of 16 ketamine infusions over 15 weeks.
: Behavioural Activation Therapy — BA therapy is a structured, primarily talk therapy that focuses on helping people with depression increase engagement in positive, meaningful activities and reduce avoidance behaviors that reinforce low mood. The BA therapy protocol will be based on the approach described by Martell et al. (2022). Therapy sessions will be delivered virtually or in person according to participant preference. Participants in Arm 1 will receive a total of 16 BA sessions at a frequency of twice weekly for 3 weeks during the Induction Phase (6 sessions), once weekly for 8 weeks during the Maintenance Phase (8 sessions), and once biweekly for 4 weeks during the Discharge Preparation Phase (2 sessions). BA therapy sessions can occur on the same day as ketamine infusions, but not during or after the infusion.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07801703

SUMMARY:

## NCT07836166
Effectiveness of Noninvasive High Frequency Oscillatory Ventilation Versus Nasal Intermittent Mandatory Ventilation as Post Extubation Respiratory Support in Neonates Born At ≥ 26 Weeks' Gestation: A Randomized Clinical Trial

Intervention: : Noninvasive High Frequency Oscillatory Ventilation — nHFOV will begin immediately after planned extubation and will be delivered through binasal prongs or a nasal mask. Initial mean airway pressure will be 10-12 cm H₂O and may be increased in 1 cm H₂O steps to a maximum of 20 cm H₂O. Initial amplitude will be 15-25 cm H₂O and may be increased in 5 cm H₂O steps to a maximum of 35 cm H₂O. Frequency will be 8-12 Hz, and the inspiratory to expiratory ratio will be 1:1. Oxygen will be adjusted to maintain oxygen saturation between 90% and 95%. Settings will be adjusted according to the participant's clinical condition.
: Nasal Intermittent Mandatory Ventilation — NIMV will begin immediately after planned extubation and will be delivered through binasal prongs or a nasal mask. Positive end expiratory pressure will be 8-12 cm H₂O, and peak inspiratory pressure will be 18-24 cm H₂O. The initial breathing rate will be 30 breaths per minute and may be increased in steps of 5 to a maximum of 50 breaths per minute according to carbon dioxide levels and clinical assessment. Inspiratory time will be 0.5 seconds. Oxygen will be adjusted to maintain oxygen saturation between 90% and 95%. Synchronization will not be used.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07836166

SUMMARY:

## NCT07857291
Efficacy of Videoconference-Based Brief Cognitive-Behavioral Therapy (V-BCBT) for Patients With Mood Disorders at High Risk for Suicide: Identifying Inflammatory Biomarkers of Suicide Risk and Examining Their Mediating Role in Treatment Effects

Intervention: : Videoconference-Based Brief Cognitive Behavioral Therapy — V-BCBT is a structured, brief cognitive behavioral intervention delivered via secure videoconferencing to outpatients with mood disorders who are at high risk for suicide. It comprises eight individual sessions of approximately 50 minutes, delivered once weekly. Treatment focuses on crisis management, development of an individualized crisis response plan, reasons for living, relaxation, sleep management, behavioral activation, cognitive restructuring, mindfulness, coping with high-risk situations, and relapse prevention. V-BCBT is provided in addition to treatment as usual (TAU), which consists of routine outpatient psychiatric care such as pharmacotherapy and supportive psychotherapy according to clinical need.
: Treatment as Usual (TAU) — Treatment as usual (TAU) refers to routine outpatient psychiatric care provided according to each participant's clinical needs and the treating clinician's judgment. It may include pharmacotherapy, supportive psychotherapy, clinical monitoring, and other standard psychiatric services ordinarily available in outpatient practice. The specific components and intensity of TAU are not standardized by the study protocol and may vary across participants based on usual clinical care.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07857291

## NCT07924436
Effects of Whole-Body Vibration Training on Ankle Muscle Activation and Functional Performance in Adults With Obesity With and Without Type 2 Diabetes

Intervention: : Whole-Body Vibration Training — Supervised whole-body vibration training will be delivered using a Hypervibe G14 Home V3 side-alternating platform twice weekly for eight weeks (16 sessions). The program comprises three stages: weeks 1-2, 3-5, and 6-8. Exercise-specific frequencies range from 6 to 30 Hz. Planned active vibration exposure per session is 12 minutes, 10.5 minutes, and 15.5 minutes across the three stages, respectively. Sessions include a 5-minute walking warm-up, platform-based exercises and vibration massage, followed by mat-based cool-down exercises. Exercise position, range of motion, support, duration, and rest will be adapted to participant tolerance within protocol limits. Borg 6-20 ratings will be recorded after each session, with a target perceived exertion of 12-14 guiding subsequent adjustments. Both groups will receive the same protocol.

## NCT07924579
The Effectiveness of a Balance Exergame Intervention on Cortical Excitability/Inhibition and Functional Performance in Elderly Fallers: A Nonblinded Randomized Controlled Trial

Intervention: : Nintendo Wii Fit Balance Exergame Training — An 8-week structured balance exergame training program using the Nintendo Wii Fit platform and balance board. Participants perform barefoot multidirectional weight-shifting exercises using five available games: Ski Slalom, Table Tilt, Tightrope Tension, Balance Bubble, and Penguin Slide. Participants may choose any available game during each session. Each supervised 60-minute session consists of a 5-minute lower-limb stretching warm-up, 50 minutes of balance exergame training, and a 5-minute cool-down with stretching exercises. Training is conducted three times per week for eight weeks. A researcher oversees the intervention, and participants may take breaks as needed.
: Conventional Balance Training — An 8-week structured conventional balance training program consisting of supervised 60-minute sessions, three times per week. Each session includes a 5-minute warm-up with lower-limb stretching, 50 minutes of balance training, and a 5-minute cool-down. The balance training comprises three components: (1) Sitting balance training (15-20 minutes), including sitting on a Swiss ball to maintain balance, moving the arms or legs, throwing and catching a ball, and kicking a ball; (2) Standing balance training (15-20 minutes), including standing with feet together, shifting body weight side to side, standing on one leg, throwing and catching a ball, and standing on a foam pad or cushion; and (3) Walking training (15-20 minutes), including marching in place, walking lunges, zigzag walking, obstacle navigation, walking on a foam pad, cognitive dual-task walking, and walking while carrying a tray of water. Training intensity is adjusted according to each participant's capability.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07924579

SUMMARY:

## NCT07924657
Sleep-Stage Acoustic Stimulation for Cognitive Improvement in Alzheimer's Disease: A Randomized Sham-Controlled Study

Intervention: : Acoustic Stimulation — Acoustic stimulation will be delivered at the patient's bedside during stable NREM sleep. The system uses a closed-loop design: a familiar, comfortable sound is triggered upon entry into NREM sleep, each stimulation lasts 30 seconds, with a 1-minute interval, and the number of stimulations is unlimited. In the experimental arm, the sounds are paired with a prior memory encoding task. In the active comparator arm, the sounds are unpaired (not presented during the encoding task), but all other acoustic parameters and NREM-triggered delivery remain identical. This distinguishes the intervention from standard acoustic stimulation or non-closed-loop auditory stimulation used in other studies.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07924657

## NCT07924813
Effects of Functional Electrical Stimulation Combined With Task-Oriented Training on Lower Limb Function, Balance, and Quality of Life in Children With Spastic Diplegic Cerebral Palsy.

Intervention: : Functional Electrical Stimulation Combined with Task- Oriented Training (FES+TOT) — Participants will receive Functional Electrical Stimulation combined with Task-Oriented Training for six weeks, with three supervised sessions per week (18 sessions in total, 60 minutes each). FES will be applied to the gluteus medius, Hamstring, quadriceps muscle using a frequency of 35 Hz, a pulse width of 250 μs, a ramp time of 1-2 seconds, and an on-to-off ratio of 1:3. Stimulation intensity will be adjusted to produce a visible, comfortable muscle contraction. Task-oriented training will include progressive functional activities such as sit-to-stand practice, standing weight shifting, level walking, stepping, obstacle negotiation, step-ups, stair training, and advanced gait activities.
: Task-Oriented Training (TOT) — Participants will receive Task-Oriented Training for six weeks, with three supervised sessions per week (18 sessions in total, 60 minutes each). Training will involve progressive, goal-directed functional activities to improve gross motor function, balance, and functional mobility. Activities will progress from sit-to-stand practice, standing weight shifting, and level walking to multidirectional stepping, obstacle crossing, step-ups, stair negotiation, changes in walking direction, variations in walking speed, and advanced gait activities according to participants' abilities.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07924813

SUMMARY:

## NCT07924917
Closed-Loop Bio-Machine Interaction System and Applications in Psychiatric Diagnosis and Treatment

Intervention: : Single-target active temporally interfering electrical stimulation — Single-target active stimulation targeting the right primary auditory cortex (A1), each target stimulation phase will last 20 minutes, including a 30-second ramp-up at onset and a 30-second ramp-down at termination. Alternating currents with frequencies of f1 = 2000 Hz and f2 = 2040 Hz will be delivered through the two electrode pairs, and the total current intensity across the two electrode pairs will be fixed at 4 mA (AC, peak-to-base). The intervention will be administered over two consecutive weeks, with participants receiving stimulation on five days per week.
: Dual-target active temporally interfering electrical stimulation — Dual-target active stimulation targeting the right A1 and the right dorsolateral prefrontal cortex (DLPFC), each target stimulation phase will last 20 minutes, including a 30-second ramp-up at onset and a 30-second ramp-down at termination. For each target, two pairs of electrodes will be used; the alternating current frequency and total stimulation intensity will be the same as those in the single-target active TI electrical stimulation group.The intervention will be administered over two consecutive weeks, with participants receiving stimulation on five days per week.
: Sham temporally interfering electrical stimulation — Sham TI electrical stimulation group will follow the same session duration, treatment frequency, and procedural setup as the active stimulation groups. However, electrical stimulation will be limited to an initial 30-second ramp-up followed immediately by a 30-second ramp-down, after which no current will be delivered for the remainder of the session.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07924917

## NCT07925073
Effectiveness of Low FODMAPs Diet Compared With Standard Dietary Advice in Functional Dyspepsia

Intervention: : Low Fodmap dietary advice — Participants assigned to the intervention arm will receive standard dietary advice for functional dyspepsia plus structured low-FODMAP dietary counseling provided by a registered dietitian. The intervention will last 2 weeks and will focus on reducing dietary intake of fermentable oligosaccharides, disaccharides, monosaccharides, and polyols (FODMAPs). Participants will receive verbal and written dietary guidance and will record their food intake throughout the intervention. Dietary adherence will be assessed by comparing estimated daily FODMAP intake during the intervention with baseline intake. Participants will complete weekly PAGI-SYM assessments and record bowel movements and any adverse effects.
: Standard dietary advice — Participants assigned to the control group will receive standardized verbal and written dietary advice for functional dyspepsia provided by a physician. They will not receive specific low-FODMAP dietary counseling and will continue their usual diet while following the general dietary recommendations. The intervention will last 2 weeks. Participants will complete weekly PAGI-SYM assessments and record their food intake, bowel movements, and any adverse effects during the study period
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07925073

SUMMARY:

## NCT07925281
Emotional Reactivity in Severe Alcohol Use Disorder and Major Depressive Disorder: a Multidimensional Approach Combining Dynamic Emotional Stimuli, Eye Tracking, Pupillometry, Electrodermal and Cardiac Activity, and Biological Measures of Stress and Interoception

Intervention: : Emotional Reactivity Experimental Protocol — Standardized experimental protocol including: (1) completion of questionnaires assessing psycho-affective, socio-emotional, stress-related, and disorder-specific factors; (2) exposure to dynamic emotional facial expressions and emotional scenes to assess emotional recognition and reactivity; (3) recording of eye-tracking, pupillometry, electrodermal activity, and cardiac activity responses; (4) assessment of bodily sensations using an emotional body map; (5) salivary and hair cortisol collection; and (6) follow-up clinical assessments at 6 and 12 months. Procedures are identical across all study groups.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07925281

## NCT07925307
C2001P-CS6 CASCADE: A Feasibility Study of HPV Screening and Management Strategies Utilizing Extended Molecular HPV DNA Genotyping and HPV Viral Load in Women Living With HIV

Intervention: : HPV DNA Testing (Abbott Alinity m HPV Assay) — Women will self-collect a vaginal specimen for primary HPV testing to be evaluated on the Abbott Alinity m platform. The Alinity m HR HPV assay is a qualitative, real-time polymerase chain reaction (PCR) assay for the detection of high-risk HPV DNA. The assay detects 14 hrHPV genotypes by targeting the conserved L1 region of HPV DNA and provides individual or group-level genotyping results, including separate readouts for HPV 16, HPV 18, and HPV 45, and grouped detection of other high-risk types. The assay also provides a proxy for HPV viral load as measured by the cycle threshold (CT) value for the type- or group-specific readout.
: Visual Inspection with Acetic Acid — Participants with hrHPV detected will undergo visual inspection with acetic acid (VIA) during a speculum examination. Standard acetic acid (3-5%) is applied to the cervix, and the provider will assess for the presence of cervical lesions, determine suitability for ablative treatment, and characterize the transformation zone (TZ1, TZ2, or TZ3). VIA findings will be used to guide participant management and treatment decisions.
: Cervical Histology (Biopsy Procedures) — Cervical histology specimens will be obtained from participants with hrHPV detected. Biopsies may include lesion-directed cervical punch biopsies, endo- and ectocervical soft-brush biopsies, or endocervical curettage (ECC). At least two biopsies will be obtained even if no lesions are seen. For participants undergoing LEEP/LLETZ, histology will be obtained through the excisional procedure. Cervical histology will be processed locally and interpreted according to the lower anogenital squamous terminology (LAST), including p16 testing where available to confirm morphologic-appearing CIN 2.
: Cervical Treatment (Thermal Ablation or LEEP/LLETZ) — Women with HPV 16 and/or 18 will undergo immediate treatment. Women with non-16/18 hrHPV will undergo immediate treatment if cervical lesions are seen on VIA. Women who are not treated but are subsequently found to have cervical high-grade squamous intraepithelial lesions (hHSIL) on histology will return for cervical treatment. Treatment modality will be determined based on the extent of cervical lesions, with thermal ablation (TA) used for lesions appropriate for ablative therapy and LEEP/LLETZ used for more extensive lesions.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07925307

SUMMARY:

## NCT07925762
Motherly Plus: Randomized Controlled Trial Evaluating a Smartphone-Assisted Single-Session Psychotherapy Intervention for Postpartum Depression

Intervention: : Smartphone-Assisted Single-Session Psychotherapy Intervention — A single online psychotherapy session (about 90 minutes) based primarily on behavioral activation: psychoeducation on the behavioral model of depression, identification of reinforcers and avoidance, collaborative scheduling of achievable value-aligned activities, problem-solving of barriers, and training in the Motherly app. Optional components (stress management, emotion regulation, cognitive restructuring, sleep hygiene) are used according to clinical judgment. A pre-session questionnaire tailors the session. Participants then use the Motherly app for 4 weeks to schedule and track activities, monitor symptoms and access modules on behavioral activation, emotion regulation, stress management, cognitive restructuring, sleep hygiene and mindfulness. The intervention is delivered by psychologists with 10 hours of training and senior supervision.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07925762

## NCT05911529
Tracking the Linguistic Signatures of Motivational Interviewing in Acute Psychosis (Former: Motivational Interviewing for Patients With Acute Psychosis)

Intervention: : Motivational Interviewing — In our study intervention, patients should receive three session of motivational interviewing (MI). Throughout the MI sessions, interviewers use common MI techniques including open-ended questions, affirmations, reflections, summaries, asking permission, expressing empathy, supporting self-efficacy, etc. Interviewers are clinical psychologists who received MI training immediately prior to the study.
: Psychoeducation — In the control intervention patients should also be given three sessions, in which no MI techniques take place. They will be carried out in the form of psychoeducational interventions, which are part of a standard treatment program for patients with schizophrenia during the acute and post-acute phases. Psychoeducation aims to inform patients about their illness and its treatment, enhancing both the understanding and the personally responsible handling of the illness and supporting those afflicted in coping with the disorder. Since we want to check whether the patients really benefit from the specific intervention rather than from receiving additional contact time, the patient in the control group will also be given three sessions. It is known that psychoeducation can have a positive effect on treatment out come by reducing relapse rate and the frequency of rehospitalizations, improving treatment ad herence, and shortening the length of inpatient hospital stays.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT05911529

SUMMARY:

## NCT06445049
Assessing Intrinsic Respiratory Rate in Mechanically Ventilated Adult Patients With Reverse Triggering as an Incentive for Accelerating Transition to Spontaneous Breathing and Extubation: Pilot Randomized Clinical Trial - INTRINSIC

Intervention: : brief test — The intervention is a simple brief test as follows: With the ventilator set with the patient clinical settings, it will be performed an up to 30sec-long or two inspiratory effort and-expiratory occlusion maneuver on the ventilator and/or switch the patient to pressure support for up to 30sec-long to assess the presence of intrinsic respiratory rate.

If an intrinsic respiratory rate ≥8 breaths per minute is present and P/F ratio is ≥150 it will be performed a 5-minute "test" in pressure support ventilation with the pressure support level to match the clinical setting of the patient. If the patient tolerates well 5 minutes in pressure support (see criteria below) the clinical team will be informed:

\- It will be indicated that the patient tolerated 5 min of PSV and suggest to the clinicians and physician in charge to transition the patient to pressure support ventilation plus suggest reducing sedation.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06445049

SUMMARY:

## NCT06603545
The Effect of Digital Education for Osteoporosis Patients on Fracture Risk and Related Health Outcomes in Older Swedish Men and Women.

Intervention: : Digital education for osteoporosis patients — DEOP is found on the Swedish health care system's dedicated website or app 1177, it takes approximately 60 minutes to complete and is composed of three modules: 1)Introduction: an animated video that shows the patient flow from having a fracture, being evaluated at the osteoporosis clinic, receiving information about osteoporosis, and being treated for osteoporosis. 2)Osteoporosis: basic information about osteoporosis; why it occurs, risk factors that the patient can affect (such as physical inactivity and smoking), and risk factors that cannot be affected (such as age and heredity). It provides information about typical osteoporotic fractures, and how a patient is evaluated according to clinical guidelines for osteoporosis care. 3)Treatment: information about the different kinds of pharmacological osteoporosis treatment, physical activity, balance control, lifting technique, risk of falling, and dietary advice, especially regarding calcium and vitamin D intake.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06603545

## NCT07470281
Oral Mucosa Wound Healing in Diabetic Patients. A Clinical Study on Closed Wounds

Intervention: : Coronally Advance Flap associated with connective tissue graft (CAF+CTG) — A CAF will be performed following the gingival recession (GR) defect features extending beyond the mucogingival junction (MGJ) in a split-full-split thickness design. In sequence, a 2-mm thick free gingival graft (FGG), measuring 15 × 5 mm, will be harvested from the palatal area between the distal of the canine and the mesial aspect of the first molar. The FGG will be trimmed based on the GR defect features and de-epithelialized to achieve a final thickness of 1 mm. The de-epithelialized graft (CTG) will be sutured in position, at the level of the cement-enamel junction (CEJ), using interrupted absorbable sutures. Finally, the flap margin will be repositioned 2 mm coronal to CEJ
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07470281

## NCT07579988
Ultrasound Measurement of Thyroid Volume in Term Newborns : a Single-centre Cross Sectional Observational Study

Intervention: : Thyroid ultrasound — Thyroid Ultrasound is a safe method because it does not involve ionizing radiation, is non-invasive and painless, takes only a few minutes, and does not cause discomfort. The Ultrasound machine is the Philips EPIQ 5 system (Philips Healthcare, Eindhoven, the Netherlands) with CE certification. The examination is conducted with high-frequency probes (linear hockey stick L12-20 MHz). All thyroid gland scans and measurements are performed by two trained observers at the bedside using a portable scanner. A generous amount of coupling gel is applied to the transducer surface, which is placed midline on the neck of the infant. The examination of each lobe is performed in both longitudinal and transverse projections. During the examination, the neonate is in the supine position with slight extension of the neck, while sucking on an assistant's gloved finger with glucose drops.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07579988
